\min\left\{\rho_{2},\rho_{3}\right\}\leq\kappa
.
Therefore, we now assume that
\rho_{2},\rho_{3}\geq\kappa
.
Note that for any
x_{1},x_{2}\geq\kappa
,

\displaystyle 0\leq 2H(x_{1}+x_{2})-H(x_{1})-H(x_{2})\leq\min_{x\geq\kappa}\left|H^{\prime\prime}(x)\right|\left|x_{1}-x_{2}\right|^{2}\leq\kappa^{-1}\left|x_{1}-x_{2}\right|^{2}.

Hence,
U(\boldsymbol{\rho})\leq U^{\mathrm{sym}}\left(\frac{\rho_{2}+\rho_{3}}{2},\rho_{4}\right)+\kappa^{-1}\eta^{2}n^{-2/3}
. By
Lemma

B.5
and the constraints of
\boldsymbol{\rho}
,

\displaystyle U^{\mathrm{sym}}\left(\frac{\rho_{2}+\rho_{3}}{2},\rho_{4}\right)\leq\max_{(\rho^{\prime}_{2},\rho^{\prime}_{4})\in\partial B\left(\left(\frac{\Delta-1}{\Delta^{2}},\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}\right),n^{-1/3}\right)}U^{\mathrm{sym}}(\rho_{2}^{\prime},\rho_{4}^{\prime})\leq U^{\mathrm{sym}}\left(\frac{\Delta-1}{\Delta^{2}},\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}\right)-\varepsilon^{\prime}n^{-2/3},

where the last inequality follows from
Lemma

B.3
and
Lemma

5.7
, and
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
is a constant only relying on
\Delta
. This concludes the proof with
\eta=\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}/2\kappa}
and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon^{\prime}/2
.
∎

Appendix C
A Refined SI Bound for the Hardcore Model

In this section, we present a refined analysis of the spectral independence for the hardcore model satisfying
\delta
-uniqueness,
proving
Lemma

3.18
.
Let
G=(V,E)
be a graph with maximum degree
\Delta\geq 3
.
Let
\mu
denote the Gibbs distribution of the hardcore model on
G
with fugacity
\lambda>0
.

For convenience, define
d:=\Delta-1
. The tree recursion for the hardcore model is defined as

\displaystyle f(x):=\lambda\left(\frac{1}{1+x}\right)^{d}.

Let
\delta^{*}\in(0,1)
be a parameter.
The hardcore model on graph
G
with fugacity
\lambda
is said to be
\delta^{*}
-unique
if the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\left|f^{\prime}(\hat{x})\right|=d\lambda\left(\frac{1}{1+\hat{x}}\right)^{d+1}=\frac{d\hat{x}}{1+\hat{x}}\leq 1-\delta^{*},

(104)

where
\hat{x}
represents the fixed point of the tree recursion (i.e., the unique positive solution of the equation
x=f(x)
).

It is worth noting that in the literature, the condition of being
\delta
-unique is often assumed for all degrees up to
\Delta
.
However, for the hardcore model, assuming
\delta
-uniqueness for degrees up to
\Delta
is equivalent to assuming it holds for the maximum degree
\Delta
.

The following spectral independence bound is known for the hardcore model that is
\delta
-unique.

Lemma C.1

(
[
24
]
)
.

If the hardcore model is
\delta^{*}
-unique, then
\mu
is
\frac{2}{\delta^{*}}\frac{\Delta\lambda}{1+\lambda}
-spectrally independent.

Remark C.2
.

In
[
24
]
, the authors introduce the notion of an
(\alpha,c)
-potential function, the existence of which implies
c/\alpha
-spectral independence for the distribution.
They show that the potential function proposed in
[
46
]
is indeed an
(\alpha,c)
-potential function with desirable parameters.
Specifically,
Lemma

C.1
is implied by combining
[
24
, Theorem 18]
(which proves
\alpha=\delta^{*}/2
) and
[
24
, Proof of Lemma 36 H.1]
(which proves
c=\frac{\lambda\Delta}{1+\lambda}
).

Proof of
Lemma

3.18
.

First, we will show that
\frac{\Delta\lambda}{1+\lambda}\leq\mathrm{e}
.
Note that
\lim_{\Delta\to\infty}\frac{\Delta\lambda_{c}(\Delta)}{1+\lambda_{c}(\Delta)}=\mathrm{e}
, and
\frac{\Delta\lambda}{1+\lambda}\leq\frac{\Delta\lambda_{c}(\Delta)}{1+\lambda_{c}(\Delta)}
.
Therefore, it suffices to show that
T(\Delta):=\frac{\Delta\lambda_{c}(\Delta)}{1+\lambda_{c}(\Delta)}
is an increasing function.

Calculating the derivative
(\log T)^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle(\log T(\Delta)^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\Delta}+\frac{(\Delta-1)\left((\Delta-2)\log\left(\frac{1}{\Delta-2}+1\right)-2\right)}{(\Delta-2)\left(\left(\frac{1}{\Delta-2}+1\right)^{\Delta}+\Delta-1\right)}.

Without loss of generality, we assume
(\Delta-2)\log(\frac{1}{\Delta-2}+1)-2\leq 0
(otherwise, the lemma holds).
Note that
(\frac{1}{\Delta-2}+1)^{\Delta}\geq 1+\frac{\Delta}{\Delta-2}+\binom{\Delta}{2}\frac{1}{(\Delta-2)^{2}}
, which follows from applying a generalization of the Bernoulli inequality
(x+1)^{r}\geq 1+rx+\frac{r(r-1)}{2}x^{2}
that holds for
r\geq 2,x\geq 0
.
We can apply this to the denominator in the above equation to obtain:

\displaystyle(\log T(\Delta)^{\prime})\geq\frac{-2\Delta+2(\Delta-2)^{2}\log\left(\frac{1}{\Delta-2}+1\right)+5}{\Delta(2\Delta-3)}.

Since
2\Delta\geq 3
, it suffices to prove:

\displaystyle\log\left(1+\frac{1}{\Delta-2}\right)\geq\frac{2\Delta-5}{2(\Delta-2)^{2}}.

By Taylor’s expansion for
\log(1+x)
at
x=0
, we have
\log(1+x)\geq x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}
for
x\in(0,1)
, leading to:

\displaystyle\log\left(1+\frac{1}{\Delta-2}\right)\geq\frac{1}{\Delta-2}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta-2}\right)^{2}=\frac{2\Delta-5}{2(\Delta-2)^{2}}.

This confirms that
T(\Delta)
is an increasing function, proving
\frac{\Delta\lambda}{1+\lambda}\leq\mathrm{e}
.

According to
Lemma

C.1
, we can complete the proof by showing that
2\delta^{*}(1+\frac{1}{\Delta-2})\geq\delta
.
The remaining proof follows a similar structure to the proof of
[
7
, Lemma C.1]
.
By (
104
), to ensure the hardcore model is
\delta^{*}
-unique we need
\hat{x}\leq\frac{1-\delta^{*}}{d-(1-\delta^{*})}
.
Since
\hat{x}=f(\hat{x})
for the fixed point
\hat{x}
, we have
\lambda=\hat{x}(1+\hat{x})^{d}
,
meaning that
\lambda
is an increasing function of the fixed point
\hat{x}
.
Thus, to make the hardcore model
\delta^{*}
-unique, it suffices to require

\displaystyle\lambda\leq\lambda^{*}:=\frac{1-\delta^{*}}{d-(1-\delta^{*})}\left(1+\frac{1-\delta^{*}}{d-(1-\delta^{*})}\right)^{d}.

By the definition of
\lambda_{c}(\Delta)=\frac{1}{d-1}\left(1+\frac{1}{d-1}\right)^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{*}}{\lambda_{c}}

\displaystyle=(1-\delta^{*})\left(1-\frac{\delta^{*}}{d-1+\delta^{*}}\right)^{d+1}

\displaystyle\geq(1-\delta^{*})\left(1-\frac{(d+1)\delta^{*}}{d-(1-\delta^{*})}\right)

(Bernoulli inequality)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\left(1+\frac{d+1}{d-(1-\delta^{*})}\right)\delta^{*}\geq 1-\left(1+\frac{d+1}{d-1}\right)\delta^{*}.

This implies that if
\delta=(1+\frac{d+1}{d-1})\delta^{*}=2(1+\frac{1}{\Delta-2})\delta^{*}
and
\lambda\leq(1-\delta)\lambda_{c}(\Delta)
, then
\lambda\leq\lambda^{*}
and the hardcore model is
\delta^{*}
-unique.
Hence, we establish that
2\delta^{*}(1+\frac{1}{\Delta-2})\geq\delta
.
This proves
Lemma

3.18
as discussed above.
∎
